*(Physical Page 73 / Leaf p75)*

## 2. Decrees of the Ruthenian Provincial Synod: Titulus I. On the Catholic Faith

### CHAPTER I. On the Profession of the Catholic Faith

I. We decree that the "Profession of Faith" according to the specimen annexed hereto [^49] for Easterners, prescribed by Pope Urban VIII, shall be made by:  
a) The members of the Provincial Synod and of the Diocesan Synod at the opening thereof;  
b) Those elevated to the Episcopate, prior to their consecration;  
c) Cathedral Dignitaries and Canons, prior to their installation;  
d) Generals and Provincials of Monastic Orders and Archimandrites, as also Monks of both sexes, when they are admitted to the Order;  
e) Professors of Theology and Philosophy who are to instruct candidates for the clerical state;  
f) Adjuncts (Assistant Lecturers) of Theology;  
g) Priests and Catechists;  
h) Deans;

---

*(Physical Page 74 / Leaf p76)*

i) Those endowed with any benefices connected with the cure of souls;  
k) Newly appointed Confessors of Nuns (Monastic Sisters);  
l) Those about to receive Holy Orders, prior to the reception of ordination.

### CHAPTER II. On Catholic Doctrine

1. Whereas "without faith it is impossible to please God" [^50], and whereas faith is the foundation and root of justification, this Synod therefore beginneth from it.  
All things, therefore, which the Catholic Church, whether by solemn judgment or by ordinary and universal magisterium, proposeth to be believed as divinely revealed, we believe, by solemn profession we confess, and we teach: namely, that which at the Ecumenical Vatican Council was pronounced in the dogmatic decrees on Catholic faith [^51], on the Church, and on the Roman Pontiff, and on His full, supreme, ordinary, and immediate jurisdiction and government over all and singular pastors and faithful, and on His infallible authority in teaching when He speaketh *ex cathedra* [^52].  
2. We declare that there must be held with the whole soul and a sincere spirit the dogmatic doctrine of Pius IX of holy memory, pronounced on the Immaculate Conception of the Mother of God in the Bull *Ineffabilis Deus* ("The Ineffable God") on the 8th day of December, 1854, solemnly promulgated in the following words: "We declare, pronounce, and define that the doctrine which holds that the most Blessed Virgin Mary, in the first instance of her conception, by a singular grace and privilege granted by Almighty God, in view of the merits of Jesus Christ, the Savior of the human race, was preserved free from all stain of original sin, is a doctrine revealed by God and therefore to be believed firmly and constantly by all the faithful" [^53].

---

*(Physical Page 75 / Leaf p77)*

3. Likewise, all and singular false opinions and doctrines that are gaining ground in our age, which the same Ecumenical Pontiff Pius IX, in his Consistorial Allocutions and in the Encyclical Letter *Quanta cura* ("How Great Solicitude") [^54] and in the annexed Syllabus of the 8th of December, 1864 [^55], by apostolic authority rejected and condemned, we in this Synod recognize and confess to be entirely rejected, and we pronounce that they must be held as rejected and condemned under penalty of the loss of salvation.  
4. We accept the Catholic doctrine concerning the duties and rights of princes and subjects, concerning the domestic family, which is the foundation of every civil society and kingdom, and concerning the right of property and possession, set forth against the errors of socialists, communists, and nihilists in the Encyclical of our Most Holy Father Pope Leo XIII of the 28th of January, 1878 [^56]; we accept and profess that all are bound to receive it, and we likewise promulgate it in this Synod.  
5. We likewise accept and command to be accepted all that the Most Holy Father Pope Leo XIII, on the Christian constitution of states, pronounced and set forth in the Encyclical *Immortale Dei* ("The Immortal God") of the 1st of November, 1885, that the nature and boundary of the rights of the Church and civil society, and her obligations in regard to religion, might be made manifest, and that Catholic people might at the same time learn what, according to the needs of these times, they ought to do in public and private affairs, and what they ought to avoid [^57]. In like manner, that which the Most Holy Pope Leo XIII taught concerning the condition of workers in the Encyclical Letter *Rerum novarum* ("Of New Things") of the 15th of May, 1891, we accept and revere [^58].  
6. In fine, we declare that all that was decreed concerning the Catholic faith at the Synod of Zamość in the year 1720 must be preserved [^59].

---

*(Physical Page 76 / Leaf p78)*

### CHAPTER III. On Religious Indifferentism

1. It is a dogma of faith that no one outside the true Church of Christ can attain eternal salvation. But the true and sole Church of Christ is that to which belongeth all that hath been so abundantly and wonderfully brought together by divine arrangement for the evident credibility of the Christian faith, since her wondrous propagation, eminent holiness, inexhaustible fruitfulness in all good things, Catholic unity, and invincible stability provide the greatest and perpetual guarantee of credibility and irrefutable proof of her divine mission. Wherefore, that abominable error which is called religious indifferentism, and which placeth the faithful on an equal footing with those who have not yet attained to the one true faith, we condemn and reject.  
2. Whereas, however, despite the perniciousness of this system of religious indifferentism, an opposing opinion hath in our times grown strong among many, even Catholics, whether through ignorance in Christian doctrine or through coldness in faith: this Synod, striving to root out this kind of hazardous error from the minds of the faithful, earnestly beseecheth them in God and admonisheth them that they preserve with great care the doctrine of the Catholic Church here set forth, since it provideth the only means of attaining the final end for which man was created; and it commandeth pastors in God that they themselves also set forth the same in their public instructions on fitting occasions, praise the grace of holy union whereby they are placed in Christ's true fold, and explain with a dignity worthy of their office the differences of dogmas that exist between the Church of Christ and that which dareth to call herself "Orthodox," lest through their negligence it should perchance happen to them that on the Day of Judgment there will be required at their hands the blood of the sheep redeemed by the precious blood of the Son of God [^60].

---

*(Physical Page 77 / Leaf p79)*

### CHAPTER IV. On Communion in Sacred Things with Heretics and Schismatics

1. Although toward non-Catholics who out of manifest perversity and malicious obstinacy rise up against the Catholic Church and her doctrine, Christ's faithful ought to conduct themselves with all circumspection and caution, and it is even fitting to avoid all familiar intercourse with them, as the Holy Spirit admonisheth us by the mouth of the holy Apostles, when He saith: *"If there come any unto you, and bring not this doctrine, receive him not into your house, neither bid him God speed: for he that biddeth him God speed is partaker of his evil deeds"* [^61]; and again: *"A man that is a heretic after the first and second admonition reject; knowing that he that is such is subverted, and sinneth, being condemned of himself"* [^62]; nevertheless, toward others who either live in good faith in their religion or plot nothing hostile against the Catholic Church, Christ's faithful are not forbidden to have friendly relations; moreover, let them strive to show them love, kindness, and goodwill in word and deed, that by this means winning them over, they may turn them away from errors and unite them with the Catholic Church.  
2. Let them take heed, however, that they never have with them such relations as are called "in sacred things" (in spiritual matters); and this the more so, because thereby non-Catholics might be confirmed in the errors of their false religion, as though by a tacit approval of the same on the part of the faithful; wherefore such communion "in sacred things" (in spiritual matters) hath been forbidden in the Church of Christ at all times, both by the canons of councils and by the decrees of the Supreme Pontiffs and the Roman Sacred Congregations, as also by the teachings of the holy Fathers. For in the canon law of the Greeks many canons are cited concerning this subject, the so-called "Apostolic Canons," namely [^63]: *"If anyone shall pray, even in a house, with an excommunicated person, let him be excommunicated"*

---

*(Physical Page 78 / Leaf p80)*

[^64]; *"If anyone, being a clergyman, shall pray together with one who is deposed, let him also be deposed"* [^65]; *"If a bishop, or presbyter, or deacon, shall only pray with heretics, let him be excommunicated; but if he hath permitted them to perform any clerical ministration, let him be deposed"* [^66].  
3. Two ancient provincial councils of the East are likewise cited there, namely: of Antioch and of Laodicea, of which the former decreed thus: *"It is not lawful to communicate with those who are excommunicated, nor to assemble in houses to pray with those who do not communicate in the Church, nor can those be received in one Church who have no part in another Church"* [^67]; and the latter thus: *"Heretics must not be permitted to enter into the house of God while they continue in heresy. It is not fitting to permit those who belong to the Church to visit the cemeteries or places called martyria (shrines of martyrs) of any heretics for the sake of prayer or veneration. But such, if they be of the faithful, shall be deprived of communion for an appointed time; yet if they repent and confess that they have sinned, it is fitting to receive them. Likewise it is not fitting to pray with heretics or schismatics"* [^68].  
As for the decrees of the Supreme Pontiffs and the Sacred Congregations, they, explicitly and expressly treating of this matter, forbid communion in sacred things with non-Catholics, as is evident from the instructions of the Congregation of the Holy Office of June 8, 1859, "On communion with heretics" (*Communicatio cum haereticis*); likewise of the year 1871 to the Archbishop of Corfu, "On the conferral of the Sacrament of Baptism" (*In collatione Sacram. baptismi*); likewise of the year 1729, "Whereas often" (*Cum saepe*); likewise of Thursday, May 10, 1753, "To the first" (*Ad primum*); and of Friday, June 1, 1866, to the Latin-rite Archbishop of Lviv, "It may be tolerated" (*Tolerari posse*); and finally of March 30, 1885, which are appended hereto in the appendix of this Synod [^69].

---

## 3. Scholarly Critical Apparatus & Footnotes

[^49]: Refers to Appendix I (*Додатокъ ч. 1*) appended to the Synodal Acts, containing the *Forma Fidei Orthodoxae* (Formula of Orthodox Faith) prescribed for Eastern Catholics under Pope Urban VIII (1642), updated to include the dogmatic definitions of the Immaculate Conception (1854) and the First Vatican Council (1870). The Synod mandates this profession of faith for all bishops prior to consecration, cathedral canons prior to installation, religious superiors, theologians, professors, pastors, catechists, deans, and candidates for Holy Orders.

[^50]: Heb. 11:6 — "But without faith it is impossible to please God: for he that cometh to God must believe that He is, and that He is a rewarder of them that diligently seek Him."

[^51]: Appendix II of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 2*), reproducing the Dogmatic Constitution on the Catholic Faith *Dei Filius* promulgated by the First Vatican Council in Session III (April 24, 1870), setting forth Catholic dogma regarding God the Creator, divine revelation, faith, and the mutual harmony between faith and reason.

[^52]: Appendix III, III-a, and III-b of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 3, 3а, 3б*), reproducing the First Dogmatic Constitution on the Church of Christ *Pastor Aeternus* promulgated by the First Vatican Council in Session IV (July 18, 1870), defining the apostolic primacy and supreme, full, ordinary, and immediate episcopal jurisdiction of the Roman Pontiff over all pastors and faithful, as well as the dogma of papal infallibility when speaking *ex cathedra*.

[^53]: Appendix IV and IV-a of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 4, 4а*), reproducing the Apostolic Constitution *Ineffabilis Deus* promulgated by Pope Pius IX on December 8, 1854, defining the dogma that the Most Blessed Virgin Mary was, from the first instant of her conception, preserved free from all stain of original sin by a singular grace and privilege of Almighty God in view of the merits of Jesus Christ, Savior of the human race.

[^54]: Appendix V of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 5*), reproducing the Encyclical Letter *Quanta cura* promulgated by Pope Pius IX on December 8, 1864, condemning modern ideological errors concerning rationalism, naturalism, religious indifferentism, and the secular state's usurpation of ecclesial authority.

[^55]: Appendix VI of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 6*), reproducing the *Syllabus of Errors* (*Syllabus complectens praecipuos nostrae aetatis errores*) issued under Pope Pius IX on December 8, 1864, cataloging eighty contemporary errors regarding pantheism, naturalism, absolute and moderate rationalism, indifferentism, socialism, secret societies, and the rights of the Church and civil power.

[^56]: Appendix VII, VII-a, and VII-b of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 7, 7а, 7б*), reproducing Pope Leo XIII's Encyclical Letter *Quod Apostolici Muneris* (December 28, 1878), condemning the tenets of socialism, communism, and nihilism, and expounding the Catholic doctrine on human society, legitimate civil authority, Christian marriage, family order, and the natural right to private property.

[^57]: Appendix VIII of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 8*), reproducing Pope Leo XIII's Encyclical Letter *Immortale Dei* (November 1, 1885) on the Christian constitution of states (*De civitatum constitutione christiana*), setting forth the divine foundation of civil society, the legitimate distinction and proper coordination between ecclesiastical and civil jurisdictions, and the duties of Catholic citizens in public life.

[^58]: Appendix IX of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 9*), reproducing Pope Leo XIII's groundbreaking social Encyclical Letter *Rerum novarum* (May 15, 1891) on the condition of the working classes (*De condicione opificum*), establishing Catholic principles regarding human labor, fair wages, workers' associations, private property, and state intervention against socio-economic exploitation.

[^59]: Appendix X of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 10*), reproducing Titulus I *De Fide Catholica* of the Ruthenian Provincial Synod of Zamość (1720), which solemnly affirmed the adherence of the Ruthenian Greek Catholic Church to Catholic dogma, the decrees of the Ecumenical Councils, and full communion with the Roman See.

[^60]: Appendix XI of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 11*), compiling canonical, patristic, and papal pronouncements against religious indifferentism, underscoring the necessity of the Catholic Church as the sole ark of salvation and the obligation of pastors to instruct the faithful in the doctrinal differences distinguishing Catholic truth from Eastern Orthodoxy.

[^61]: 2 John 10–11 — "If there come any unto you, and bring not this doctrine, receive him not into your house, neither bid him God speed: for he that biddeth him God speed is partaker of his evil deeds."

[^62]: Titus 3:10–11 — "A man that is a heretic after the first and second admonition reject; knowing that he that is such is subverted, and sinneth, being condemned of himself."

[^63]: Apostolic Canon 10: "If any one shall pray, even in a private house, with an excommunicated person, let him also be excommunicated" (in Byzantine canonical collections: *Εἴ τις ἀκοινωνήτῳ, κἂν ἐν οἴκῳ, συνεύξηται, οὗτος ἀφοриζέσθω*).

[^64]: Apostolic Canon 11: "If any clergyman shall join in prayer with a deposed clergyman, as a clergyman, let him also be deposed" (*Εἴ τις καθῃρημένῳ κληρικῷ, ὡς κληρικὸς συνεύξηται, καθαιρείσθω καὶ αὐτός*).

[^65]: Apostolic Canon 45 (numbered 44 in certain Western canonical editions and manuscripts, including Hardouin): "Let a bishop, presbyter, or deacon, who has only prayed with heretics, be excommunicated; but if he has permitted them to perform any clerical ministration, let him be deposed" (*Ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ πρεσβύτερος, ἢ διάκονος, αἱρετικοῖς συνευξάμενος μόνον, ἀφοриζέσθω· εἰ δὲ καὶ ἐπέτρεψεν αὐτοῖς ὡς κληρικοῖς ἐνεργῆσαί τι, καθαιρείσθω*).

[^66]: Jean Hardouin, S.J. (*Joannes Harduinus*), *Acta Conciliorum et Epistolae Decretales ac Constitutiones Summorum Pontificum* (Paris, 1715), Vol. I, cols. 12 and 21, collecting the Canons of the Apostles from Greek and Latin canonical codices.

[^67]: Council of Antioch in Encaeniis (A.D. 341), Canon 2: Forbidding ecclesiastical communion or private house-prayer with those cut off from Church communion; Hardouin, *Acta Conciliorum*, Vol. I, col. 593.

[^68]: Council of Laodicea (4th century), Canons 6, 9, and 33: Canon 6 forbids heretics to enter into the house of God while remaining in heresy; Canon 9 forbids Catholics from frequenting heretical cemeteries or alleged shrines of martyrs (*martyria*) for prayer; Canon 33 expressly commands: "One must not join in prayer with heretics or schismatics" (*Ὅτι οὐ δεῖ αἱρετικοῖς ἢ σχισмаτικοῖς συνεύχεσθαι*); Hardouin, *Acta Conciliorum*, Vol. I, cols. 781, 785.

[^69]: Appendix XII and XII-a, b, c, d of the Synodal Acts (*Додатокъ ч. 12, 12а, 12б, 12в, 12г*), collecting normative instructions and responsa of the Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Holy Office (*Sanctum Officium*) regulating *communicatio in sacris* with non-Catholics: the General Instruction of June 8, 1859 (*Communicatio cum haereticis*); the Rescript of 1871 to the Archbishop of Corfu regarding baptismal sponsorship and administration (*In collatione Sacramenti baptismi*); the Decree *Cum saepe* of 1729; the Responsum *Ad primum* of Thursday, May 10, 1753; the Rescript *Tolerari posse* of Friday, June 1, 1866, addressed to the Latin Archbishop of Lviv; and the Holy Office Instruction of March 30, 1885.

<!-- START COHORT 1891_synod_cohort7 -->

# Acts and Decrees of the Ruthenian Provincial Synod of Lviv (1891)
## Tier 1 (The Calibration Anchor) — Cohort 7: Physical Pages 81–100 (Leaves p81–p100)

> [!NOTE]
> **Historical & Canonical Significance**  
> Cohort 7 of the **1891 Lviv Provincial Synod** (*Чинности и рѣшеня руского провинціяльного Собора въ Галичинѣ ôтбувшого ся во Львовѣ въ роцѣ 1891*) encompasses two core legislative pillars of the Galician Greek-Catholic Church at the close of the 19th century:  
> 1. **Conclusion of Titulus I: On the Catholic Faith (Leaves p81–p92 / Book pp. 79–90)**:  
>    - **Chapter IV Conclusion: On Communion in Sacred Things with Heretics and Schismatics (Leaf p81 / Book p. 79)**: Reaffirmation of the 1720 Synod of Zamość's strict prohibition against *communicatio in sacris*; pastoral warning and censure against lay attendance at schismatic divine services and the payment of Mass stipends to schismatic clergy for memorial services of deceased national figures (e.g., poets and politicians).  
>    - **Chapter V: On the Censorship of Books (Leaves p81–p84 / Book pp. 79–82)**: Natural law foundation of ecclesiastical censorship; apostolic precedent at Ephesus (Acts 19:19); establishment of the Sacred Congregation of the Index by St. Pius V; systematization of excommunications *latae sententiae* reserved to the Holy See under Pope Pius IX's Constitution *Apostolicae Sedis* (1869); warning against secular rationalism, curiosity, and commercial gain; Pope Gregory XVI's 1841 Apostolic Letter to Metropolitan Mykhailo Levytsky forbidding liturgical books printed at Russian schismatic presses; mandatory episcopal approvals for prayerbooks, theological treatises, and parochial libraries; pastoral vigilance regarding hostile periodicals.  
>    - **Chapter VI: On the Sect of Freemasons and Other Condemned or Forbidden Societies (Leaves p84–p87 / Book pp. 82–85)**: Condemnation of secret societies and Freemasonry under Pope Leo XIII; excommunication reserved to the Roman Pontiff; canonical obligation of denouncing secret coryphaei to ecclesiastical authority (Holy Office Responsum of 1871); vigilance against oath-bound fraternal groups and deceptive philanthropic organizations (Holy Office Instruction of May 10, 1884); pre-sacramental pledges for youth; warning against magnetism, spiritism, divination, and sorcery; promotion of Catholic guilds for artisans and agriculturalists under heavenly patron saints.  
>    - **Chapter VII: On Preaching the Word of God (Leaves p87–p90 / Book pp. 85–88)**: Scriptural mandate of preaching (Isa. 61:1 / Luke 4:18, Mark 16:15, 2 Tim. 4:2); Tridentine conciliar legislation (Sess. V, XXIII, XXIV); Ruthenian precedents from Zamość (1720) and pastoral letters of Metropolitan Spyrydon Lytvynovych (1865) and Bishop Ivan Stupnytsky (1873); mandatory Sunday and feast-day preaching; homilies at early Liturgy; homiletic content (Sunday Gospels, Catechism, dogmatics, morals, and liturgy); preparation and sacred decorum on the ambo; citation of scripture from approved Church Slavonic editions accompanied by vernacular translation; strict prohibition of airing personal disputes, injuries, political partisanship, or public shaming of individual sinners from the ambo (Council of Vienna 1858); invocation of the Holy Spirit; moral purity of the preacher (1 Cor. 9:27, Bonaventure's *Pharetra*, Council of Prague).  
>    - **Chapter VIII: On the Catechism (Leaves p90–p92 / Book pp. 88–90)**: Foundational importance of childhood religious formation; mandatory catechetical instruction after Vespers on Sundays/feasts, in daughter churches, and in elementary schools; prescribed textbooks; biblical history; intensive Lenten instruction for First Confession and Solemn Communion; daily prayers; pastoral duty to admonish negligent parents and employers (Eichstätt Pastoral Instruction); Metropolitan Joseph Sembratovych's 1876 pastoral letter; eternal pastoral recompense (2 Tim. 4:7–8).  
> 2. **Promulgation of Titulus II: On the Mysteries and Their Administration (Leaves p93–p100 / Book pp. 91–98)**:  
>    - **Synodal Preamble (Leaf p93 / Book p. 91)**: Reaffirmation of the 1720 Synod of Zamość and the 1863 *Concordia*; urgent statutory mandate for the Metropolitan to publish an official, uniform Ruthenian *Trebnik* (Ritual) conforming to the synodal schema.  
>    - **Chapter I: On the Holy Mystery of Baptism (Leaves p93–p94 / Book pp. 91–92)**: Time of administration (without delay, Eugenius IV *Cantate Domino*); ordinary place (parish church with font, Clementine Constitutions) with winter domestic exception in the parish house; mode of administration (preserving Ruthenian triple infusion / pouring); prohibition of separating baptism from accompanying rites; canonical qualifications and moral obligations of godparents (*kumy*, Synod of Ferrara 1751); appendix rubric schema.  
>    - **Chapter II: On the Holy Mystery of Chrismation (Leaves p94–p95 / Book pp. 92–93)**: Annual renewal of Holy Chrism (*sviate Myro*) consecrated on Great and Holy Thursday; decanal distribution within thirty days; burning of old Chrism on a clean stone and deposition of ashes into the *Sacrarium*; mandatory construction of parochial *Sacraria* for ashes and sacred ablution waters; official schema for the new *Trebnik*.  
>    - **Chapter III: On the Most Holy Mystery of the Eucharist (Leaves p95–p98 / Book pp. 93–96)**: Dogmatic definition affirming that the Words of Institution alone ("This is My Body... This is My Blood") effect Eucharistic transubstantiation, refuting contemporary schismatic errors attributing consecration solely to the Epiklesis; patristic authorities (St. John Chrysostom, St. Irenaeus, St. Gregory of Nyssa) and Council of Florence; liturgical uniformity in reciting the Words of Institution; vestments for carrying Holy Viaticum (epitrachelion and phelonion, updating Zamość); communal prayers before communion; prohibition of interpolating "unworthy" into the distribution formula; communion under both kinds during Liturgy; prohibition of adding unblessed wine to the chalice; communion from reserved particles for the sick, pilgrims arriving late fasting, and outside Liturgy; wiping of mouth with communion cloth by priest or deacon; extension of the Paschal communion period from Clean Monday through the Feast of the Ascension; pastoral denunciation of non-communicants to the Ordinary for excommunication.  
>    - **Chapter IV: On the Mystery of Repentance (Leaves p99–p100 / Book pp. 97–98)**: Canonical tradition of confessing at least three times annually (Pascha, Dormition, Nativity); liberty to make annual confession before any approved priest while receiving Paschal communion in one's own parish; absolute prohibition of charging confession fees, selling confession tickets (*kartky do spovidi*), or demanding parish dues (*proskurne*); bi-ritual confessional faculties under the 1863 *Concordia*; distribution of lists of reserved cases (*Apostolicae Sedis* and diocesan tables); special faculties required for confessors of nuns; mandatory installation of confessional booths (*spovidalnytsi*) in churches.

---

## 1. Decrees of the Ruthenian Provincial Synod: Titulus I. On the Catholic Faith (Continued)

*(Physical Page 79 / Leaf p81)*

### Chapter IV: On Communion in Sacred Things with Heretics and Schismatics (Conclusion)

Further, that doctrine of the Catholic Church concerning communion in sacred things with non-Catholics was also clearly set forth by our Synod of Zamość, which decided thus: *"Moreover, it is not permitted to anyone, of whatever state or station he may be, who professeth the Catholic faith, to go unto outside pastors and their churches, whether for the reception of the Mysteries or out of devotion, lest it appear that they have actual communion with those who are without; in the contrary case, Ordinaries shall proceed against them as against suspects."* [^70]

3. Whereas, however, the present Synod hath learned not without great sorrow of its heart that certain laymen at times depart from that doctrine, so that in the church of schismatics during sacred functions together with them, without scruple of conscience, they are observed to be present, and at times also do not hesitate to give stipends for sacred services for the help of the soul of some Ruthenian poet or some political man of greater renown, a schismatic, unto schismatic priests, and seem to regard this as permitted, or at least as indifferent; therefore it strictly admonisheth all priests, catechists of public schools, as also other pastors of souls, especially where occasion presenteth itself, that they duly instruct the faithful committed to their care concerning this matter, and fail not to deter them from that custom, which is contrary to the spirit of Christ's Church, since such communion with those of other faiths in sacred things would prepare undoubted harm for their souls.

---

### CHAPTER V. On the Censorship of Books

1. By the very law of nature the reading of those books whose use bringeth the danger of sin is forbidden. Therefore from the very beginning of the Church, Bishops, and above all the Roman Pontiff, have always the duty, and to them

*(Physical Page 80 / Leaf p82)*

pertaineth the right to prohibit the reading of bad books and periodicals; for they are appointed pastors of the flock of God [^71]; and the duty of a pastor is to go before his sheep [^72] and lead them unto good pastures, and to restrain them from bad and harmful pastures, whether they be delivered by word or in writing. Wherefore already the Apostles strove by their authority to tear bad books from the hands of the faithful, just as, according to the testimony of Saint Luke [^73], many Ephesians who followed curious arts brought together their books and burned them before the eyes of all. Following the example of the Apostles, their successors, the Catholic Bishops, in every age condemned evil writings and strove to uproot them.

2. Greater vigilance in this matter Bishops exercised from the time when, through the invention of printing, books began to be disseminated with great rapidity. — And that the condemnations of books might become more easily known to the faithful, and that the reading of forbidden books might be more safely prevented, through the care and solicitude of the Roman Pontiffs there was compiled an Index of Forbidden Books, and church censures or penalties were prescribed and established against transgressors of the prohibitions of censorship. In addition, Saint Pius V established a special "Congregation of the Index" (*Congregatio Indicis*), which hath the duty to examine books infected with the poison of heresy, permeated with doctrine harmful to religion and good morals, and to prohibit the dissemination, keeping, and reading of such books, subjecting those who act to the contrary to excommunication [^74]. That Congregation therefore from time to time inscribeth works harmful to faith and morals into the Index of Forbidden Books and deterreth from the reading of the same. In more recent times, however, the Supreme Pontiff Pope Pius IX of blessed memory abolished, or rather systematized, the earlier prescriptions regarding censures, imposed by the Constitution *Apostolicae Sedis* ("Of the Apostolic See") of October 12, 1869, in Section 2, against those who would not cease to read forbidden books, and decreed that all and singular, who knowingly without permission of the Apostolic See read the books of apostates and heretics defending heresy, as also the books of whatever author

*(Physical Page 81 / Leaf p83)*

specifically forbidden by Apostolic Letters, and who retain, print, and in any way defend those same books, shall incur excommunication of judgment passed (*latae sententiae*), in a special manner reserved to the Roman Pontiff.

3. On that foundation the present Synod admonisheth pastors constantly and unceasingly to prevent the dangers to faith and morals arising from the reading of bad books, brochures, and periodicals. Wherefore let them strive to prohibit the doctrines of those of other faiths, and to see that books containing heresy or schism, or offending against the purity of morals, or superstitious, or in any way contrary to public order, be not sown among the faithful. There are indeed those who deem it an unjust thing to prohibit people from reading any books under the pretext of desiring to know everything; but these are drawn and deceived by the whisperings of the ancient serpent, who under pretext of bringing our first parents to the knowledge of everything, urged them to pluck the forbidden fruit with the words: *"Ye shall be as gods, knowing good and evil"* [^75]. Others deem it unbearable not to have books that satisfy curiosity and bring something new to an insatiable spirit, as if Christians ought to suffer nothing burdensome, and as if the words of the Savior and Lord had no meaning: *"If thine eye offend thee, pluck it out, and cast it from thee"* [^76]. There are also not a few who support themselves by scribbling, and for the sake of obtaining gain labor in the writing, printing, and selling of subversive books; but these, having faith and good morals for sale, and enriching their estate from the death of souls, forget the words of the Savior in Saint Matthew: *"What is a man profited, if he shall gain the whole world, and lose his own soul? or what shall a man give in exchange for his soul?"* [^77]. Wherefore the present Synod, renewing the decisions and enactments concerning forbidden books issued by the Synod of Zamość, as found at the end of Title I, "On the Catholic Faith," commandeth all that for church use they acquire and admit only such editions of liturgical books as

*(Physical Page 82 / Leaf p84)*

are printed with the permission of the legitimate Ordinary (Bishop). To memory must be recalled here the words addressed by Pope Gregory XVI of blessed memory to Metropolitan Mykhailo Levytsky on the 17th of July, 1841: *"With a vigilant spirit continually take heed that this clergy and people (Ruthenian) receive not Sluzhebniks or Catechisms, or any other liturgical and religious books whatever, that have issued from schismatic presses."* The same applieth also to printed prayer books for the use of the laity, namely that they use no other books of devotion except those that have been printed with the permission of ecclesiastical authority. In addition, the same Synod commandeth that in the future theological books and any others treating directly of religion shall not, without the prior approval of the Bishop, be published or read by persons of whatever estate. The parish, decanal, and eparchial library shall be provided only with such theological, homiletic, and catechetical works wherein the approval of the Superiors of the Catholic Church is explicitly found.

4. In fine, this our Synod admonisheth priests that they lead the laity by their own example, and that they read not bad periodicals, hostile to the faith and the Catholic Church, and especially those prohibited by ecclesiastical authority, and that they strive with all their might to turn away the faithful committed to their pastoral care from reading them. — If, however, any priest for a just cause should need to read forbidden books, let him not do so without the knowledge and permission of his Ordinary, who will be able to grant priests such permission according to the faculties received from the Holy See.

---

### CHAPTER VI. On the Sect of Freemasons and Other Condemned or Forbidden Societies

1. Already from the beginning of the Church, by the permission of Christ the Lord, who explicitly foretold: *"It must needs be that

*(Physical Page 83 / Leaf p85)*

offences come"* [^78], subtle men arose who, mingling divine things with human, sowed tares in the field of the Church. Worst among others arose those who flatter human passions and seduce the uneducated and simple, striving with all their might under the guise of honesty and beneficence to overturn the firm and unshakable principles of the Catholic faith and divine revelation, as well as the foundations of civil society. And that these men might more easily attain their subversive purpose, they joined together in secret societies, which go under the name of Freemasons or under other names.

2. Against this kind of secret societies the Roman Pontiffs issued very many constitutions and apostolic decrees, and most recently our Most Holy Father Pope Leo XIII exposed their murderous teachings, contrary to Christ and His Church, and their secret aims for the destruction of both civil and ecclesiastical society, and at the same time seasonably instructed the faithful by what means they may guard themselves against their deception and snares.

3. Yet the Roman Pontiffs were not content merely to condemn and publicly excommunicate them, but willed also that those who in any degree adhere to them, or show them any favor, should incur excommunication reserved to the Pope [^79]. Therefore let all those who strive to avoid shipwreck concerning faith and eternal salvation shun this kind of sects, and those who have unhappily fallen into them, let them separate themselves from them without delay.

4. Sectarians of this kind, and likewise all the faithful cognizant of the matter, are strictly bound to denounce to the Church—that is, unto him who by reason of his pastoral office is bound to watch and guard lest the sheep committed to him fall among wolves—the members of an excommunicated society; and under censure reserved to the Roman Pope, the secret coryphaei and leaders of those sects, which censure upon denunciation ceaseth [^80].

*(Physical Page 84 / Leaf p86)*

5. Besides these, there are yet other societies that work against the Church or legitimate authorities, whether secretly or openly, under oath or without an oath concerning the keeping of secrecy, which are forbidden and which under threat of grave guilt must be avoided; among these must be numbered above all those societies that demand of their adherents under oath not to disclose secrets to anyone and to obey secret leaders. Likewise, there are found here and there societies that, although they seem to differ from the above-mentioned, are nevertheless questionable and full of danger both on account of the doctrines they profess and on account of the manner of conduct their leaders maintain. Against these also the Holy Synod warneth pastors, fathers of families, and other righteous men, that they deter and restrain everyone, but especially the youth, and that the more carefully, since on account of a certain appearance of honesty and even beneficence maintained by them, the danger of corruption hidden within them cannot so easily be perceived and guarded against [^81].

6. Likewise this Synod decreeth that pastors in teaching the catechism frequently explain what kinds of societies these are and whither they tend. And those who instruct the youth for the reception of the Holy Mysteries, let them lead them to promise beforehand that they will never enter any society without taking counsel of their parents, pastor, or confessor.

7. We likewise admonish pastors of souls that they warn the faithful committed to them with all diligence, both against practicing and against having recourse for counsel to that kind of sorceries known under the name of magnetism, spiritism, divination, and witchcraft, which have already and in such manner been forbidden by the Church.

8. Let the Most Reverend Bishops strive diligently that in individual parishes societies of artisans and agriculturalists, for piety and mutual

*(Physical Page 85 / Leaf p87)*

assistance, having chosen for themselves a heavenly Patron, be founded, and that from day to day they grow ever more in the number of their members.

---

### CHAPTER VII. On Preaching the Word of God

1. Since the Word of God is spiritual bread to the soul, indispensably necessary for man unto the attainment of eternal salvation, therefore it is manifest that this apostolic office of preaching the Word of God pertaineth to the chief duties of pastors of souls, because they further fulfill that same mission which the Son of God Himself incarnate received from His heavenly Father, as He Himself saith according to the prophet Isaiah: *"The Spirit of the Lord is upon Me, because He hath anointed Me to preach the gospel to the poor; He hath sent Me... to preach the acceptable year of the Lord, and the day of recompense"* [^82]; and which He enjoined upon the Apostles and their successors in the apostolic office after His glorious Resurrection, saying: *"Go ye into all the world, and preach the gospel to every creature"* [^83]. In obedience to that command, the Apostles not only themselves fearlessly fulfilled this office even unto death, but also strictly enjoined the same upon the pastors appointed by them, and among them most of all Paul, saying unto Timothy: *"Preach the word; be instant in season, out of season; reprove, rebuke, exhort with all longsuffering and doctrine"* [^84].

2. Wherefore it hath at all times lain closest to the heart of the Church to lay this commandment upon pastors of souls, whether by laws or by admonitions; and most solemnly the Fathers of the Council of Trent thought fit to recall this duty to the memory of pastors of souls [^85].

3. In particular, regarding our Church, this command was emphatically enjoined at our first

*(Physical Page 86 / Leaf p88)*

Provincial Synod [^86], and thereafter in many pastoral letters of the Ruthenian Bishops [^87].

4. Therefore the present Synod commandeth pastors of souls that at least on every Sunday and on feast days, they themselves never omit to preach the Word of God suitably to their own understanding and that of their people, unless they be legitimately hindered. Those pastors to whom assistants are assigned, let them see that they do not cast upon them the entire burden of the preaching office, nor that they entirely exclude them from the fulfillment of that office, but that by turns, together with them, they apply themselves to the duty of preaching the Word of God. In the parishes of larger towns, where there are more priests, the pastor shall see to it that the faithful who are able to be present only at the earlier Divine Liturgy on Sundays and feast days, be nourished at that same Divine Liturgy with the Word of God in a short homiletic instruction.

5. Since the purpose of preaching is to enlighten the minds of the faithful and move their hearts to the pursuit of virtues and the avoidance of errors, let them choose as the subject of their sermons dogmatic and moral truths, often also liturgical ones for the connection of rites with dogmas and morals, as also historical ones, in so far as they relate to dogmas and morals; but above all let them direct their diligence toward drawing the subject of their preaching from the appointed Gospels of Sundays or feasts, whose mysteries let them clearly set forth and explain, and also from Christian doctrine contained in the catechism.

6. As to the manner of their sermons, above all let preachers take care that they go not up to the ambo without due preparation, and thus proclaim whatever first cometh into their heads, without any order and arrangement; and in general let them guard lest they set before the faithful anything confused, not pertaining to the subject of the sermon suitable for the day; since

*(Physical Page 87 / Leaf p89)*

from such a sermon not only could the faithful derive no benefit, but the preacher himself would call down upon himself the wrath of God speaking through the Prophet: *"Cursed be he that doeth the work of the Lord negligently"* [^88]. Let preachers observe that commendable custom recognized among us as best, to cite at the head of the sermon a text containing, as it were in seed, the entire sermon, and that in the liturgical language; where, however, such a text happeneth to be difficult to understand, let them immediately subjoin a translation of it in the vernacular tongue; and let them do likewise wherever in the course of the sermon texts of Sacred Scripture are to be proclaimed; these, however, must be taken from the authentic Slavonic edition approved by the Catholic Church; then let the speech of the sermon always be vernacular, that is, accessible to the understanding of the people, yet not disfigured by vulgar expressions. Further, let them take heed not to repeat too frequently one and the same thing to the people. In addition: *"Let the priest preaching Christ be filled with the affairs of Christ, and think not even of his own; let him hold to the rule that it is never fitting to speak of himself, and let him not easily persuade himself that he hath a just cause for an exception. Concerning an injury which he hath suffered or thinketh he hath suffered, let him not complain before the parishioners from the sacred elevation (the ambo). He will fulfill his duty if he discuss more thoroughly the sins that more frequently occur among his people, stand up against their abomination, recommend the rewards and beauty of the contrary virtue, but never let him describe the scandals of individual persons in such a manner that withholding the name will be of little avail. Let the pastor summon to himself the man through whom scandal came among the people, and let him confer with him privately, or let him use the assistance of the village elders. Public reproof of sinners, according to the circumstances of the time, is wont rather to embitter them than to incline them unto salvation, and great contentions and difficulties may even arise therefrom"* [^89]. Therefore let the preacher strictly beware lest in

*(Physical Page 88 / Leaf p90)*

his discourses to the people he speak either of himself, or of his private affairs, or of matters purely political and secular, which have no connection with the doctrine of faith and good morals; and equally let him not revile publicly and by name, nor censure any persons whatever, even private ones, and much less superiors and men distinguished by public authority.

7. And that unto the salvific fulfillment of that duty of preaching, preachers may be duly animated and inspired in spirit, let them fail not to invoke the Teacher of truth and the Comforter, who alone hath the keys and openeth when He willeth and is able, by His internal operation, to enter into the souls of the faithful and by His divine power penetrate the hearts of the hearers. Further, let them frequently awaken within themselves the pure and holy resolution that they seek the glory of God alone, and not their own, and no other reward for their labor than the salvation of souls.

8. Unto that end, constantly turning their eyes upon their own weakness, let them earnestly beware *"lest by any means, when they have preached to others, they themselves should be castaways"* [^90]. Finally, that they may be able the more surely to trust that, fulfilling their ministry duly, they will merit a portion with those who from the beginning have wielded the Word of God well: let them not cease to shepherd and teach the flock of the Lord not only by word, but also by their own example. *"For great is the condemnation of him who ordereth his discourse well, but neglecteth his own life and his own works"* [^91].

---

### CHAPTER VIII. On the Catechism

1. Since the Catechism aimeth especially at the instruction of youth in the doctrine of salvation, and since this age is most inclined both unto good and unto evil, and longest retaineth the first teachings received from childhood,

*(Physical Page 89 / Leaf p91)*

so that the direction given in that age is the foundation of the religious way of life in mature age, therefore the Synod deemeth it its duty to turn the greatest attention of pastors unto that most chief duty, that they apply themselves unto it with the utmost exactitude, as unto a very powerful means whereby the faithful committed to their solicitude are most successfully directed unto an honorable life. Let pastors of souls therefore know that they are bound in conscience, and that under grave accountability before God, to teach and expound the principles of Christian doctrine not only in church on every Sunday and feast day immediately after Vespers, and in daughter churches immediately after that divine service which they have celebrated therein, but also in schools according to the regulations issued by the Ordinary, and that above all on account of the present system adopted in modern schools.

2. In expounding the Catechism they shall follow the order of that textbook which is temporarily prescribed by the Ordinary as the rule to be used in elementary schools, so that in the course of a year they may complete the entire subject matter of the Catechism. To the Catechism let them also join the principal events of Biblical history that have a special connection with the dogmas of faith and morals. In the season of the Great Fast (Forty Days), let them choose for their instructions the doctrine concerning the Mysteries of Repentance and the Most Holy Eucharist, that they may worthily prepare the youth, especially those who are to receive those Mysteries for the first time, for the reception of them; and let them strive that they learn all that must be explicitly believed for the attainment of eternal salvation. Let them also turn their attention to this, that the youth learn the daily prayers correctly, so that they interpolate nothing unnecessary, and neither corrupt nor omit words.

3. In addition, this Synod remindeth pastors of souls that it is their duty to admonish fathers and mothers to send their children and domestic servants at the appointed times to church for instruction in the Catechism, and to teach them that they are strictly bound to do so, since they ought not only to care for their temporal good, but much more for their spiritual and eternal good; the pastor himself ought to go before them by his own example, sending also his own domestics

*(Physical Page 90 / Leaf p92)*

to church for the Catechism. And if any parents or guardians show themselves negligent in sending their dependents, let him summon them to himself, that with paternal firmness he may remind them of this so sacred a duty [^92].

4. Finally, this Synod, desiring that pastors of souls undertake and perform catechesis with all diligence both in church and in school—for they must know that this is their first and greatest duty, and therefore any negligence or omission in this matter, however slight, yet culpable, inflicteth great harm upon the parish—obligeth them before all things to the greatest diligence and zeal in teaching the youth; and unto that end, besides what hath been set forth above, it also recalleth to their memory the most luminous instructions and regulations that were enjoined concerning this office and duty by the pastoral letter of the Most Reverend Metropolitan Joseph Sembratovych to the clergy of the Archeparchy of Lviv on the 15th of December, 1876 [^93].

5. For having duly preserved and conscientiously fulfilled the above-mentioned regulations, they may hope not only for a hundredfold fruit from the seed of the Word of God sown by them, but they themselves will surely become partakers of that reward wherewith the Lord will crown the labors bestowed by them in explaining the Catechism; for the rest, already on this earth they will rejoice in that comfort and satisfaction which the Apostle of the Nations experienced when, hoping in the goodness of Christ the Lord, he said: *"I have fought a good fight... henceforth there is laid up for me a crown of righteousness"* [^94]. Especially when catechesis, diligently and in a due manner imparted, will effect that as many spiritual sons as possible are formed unto the glory of God, the consolation of the pastor himself and parents, and unto the crown of an honorable Christian in the future, and unto the adornment of the parish, through whose care religion and piety will ever flourish among the faithful of the parish.

---

## 2. Decrees of the Ruthenian Provincial Synod: Titulus II. On the Mysteries and Their Administration

*(Physical Page 91 / Leaf p93)*

### Preamble: Mandate for the Official Ruthenian Trebnik

Retaining without change and in its full force all that the Synod of Zamość [^95] pronounced concerning the dignity and manner of administering the Holy Mysteries, as also that which is prescribed in the Decree of the Sacred Congregation for the Propagation of the Faith promulgated under the name *Concordia* ("Agreement") of the 5th of October, 1863, which is annexed hereto in the appendix [^96], this Synod decreeth:

Whereas up to this time the wise regulation of the said Synod of Zamość concerning the publication of a new *Trebnik* (Ritual), on the basis of which all priests of our rite might observe uniformity in the administration of the Holy Mysteries, hath not yet been satisfied in all its parts, the Most Reverend Metropolitan shall take care that such an edition, with all possible diligence and exactness, according to the exemplar prescribed by this Synod, be printed as soon as possible.

---

### CHAPTER I. On the Holy Mystery of Baptism

Concerning the administration of Holy Baptism, the present Synod decreeth that unto what the Synod of Zamość prescribed, the following be added:

1. a) *As to the time*: Pastors shall endeavor that it be administered as soon as can possibly be according to circumstances [^97].  
b) *As to the place*: That the same be ordinarily administered in church [^98]. In our regions, however, on account of the severity of the climate, especially in the winter season, the Synod of Zamość permitteth that the same may at times be administered also in private houses (Tit. III, § 1); according however to the custom now existing

*(Physical Page 92 / Leaf p94)*

in the said cases the child to be baptized is wont to be brought to the parish house, which custom, according to the judgment of the present Synod, may be retained.  
c) *As to the manner*: Above all the Synod decreeth that the custom received among us from time immemorial, of baptizing by triple pouring (infusion), must be retained.

2. That, except in case of extreme necessity, baptism be not separated from the ceremonies attached thereto in the Ritual (*Ustav*).

3. That the pastor pay heed to the choice of sponsors (godparents / *kumy*), as is already prescribed in the Synod of Zamość (Tit. III, § 1); in particular, let the pastor of souls instruct the sponsors that they are placed in the room of parents, and it properly belongeth unto them to instill into the children whom they held at baptism the precepts of faith and good morals, if their parents should either neglect to do so, or be overtaken by death, or by reason of their absence or in any other way hindered, be unable to do so of themselves [^99].

For the rest, for the introduction of perfect uniformity in the administration of this as of all the other Holy Mysteries, the Synod annexeth hereto a schema both of the text and of the ceremonies, which ought to serve as an exemplar for the publication of the new *Trebnik*, which all are bound strictly to observe [^100].

---

### CHAPTER II. On the Holy Mystery of Chrismation

Concerning the Holy Mystery of Chrismation, the Synod commandeth pastors of souls that they preserve all that the Synod of Zamość prescribed regarding this Holy Mystery; in particular, as to the matter of this Mystery, that is, the Holy Chrism (*sviate Myro*), the present Synod insisteth that the same be renewed yearly at the appointed time. But whereas in this regard negligence of pastors is at times observed, the Synod strictly admonisheth them that they observe this prescription exactly;

*(Physical Page 93 / Leaf p95)*

and therefore immediately after the consecration of the new Chrism, which ordinarily taketh place on Great and Holy Thursday, they shall procure the same through their respective decanal office, and it shall be used only until the consecration of the new Chrism of the following year, which is to be performed on Great and Holy Thursday, so that within a month from the consecration they may already have the new Chrism in their possession; and the strict duty of watching over this is laid upon the respective Deans themselves. The Holy Chrism the Deans themselves, or priests commissioned by them, are ordinarily bound to receive from the Prefect of the Cathedral Sacristy and deliver to the decanate.

But whereas the Synod of Zamość prescribeth that the old Chrism be burned upon a clean stone, and its ashes cast into a sacred place (*Sacrarium*) [^101], therefore the present Synod admonisheth pastors, all and singular, that in their churches they have such sacred places (*Sacraria*), and where there are none, that they hasten as quickly as possible to construct such, into which shall be cast not only these ashes, but also those from other sacred things, and where also shall be poured out the water that hath served either for Holy Baptism already administered, or for the washing of hands after Chrismation and other holy anointings.

As to the text and rubrics (*ustav*) of this Holy Mystery, the new edition shall conform to the outline which is here annexed and approved by the Synod [^102].

---

### CHAPTER III. On the Most Holy Mystery of the Eucharist

1. Retaining all that concerning the matter and form of this Mystery, as necessary to be known and done, was prescribed in the Synod of Zamość, we omit not especially to emphasize anew that all priests hold and firmly believe that the words consecrating this Mystery are no other than the words of the Lord alone: *"Take, eat: this is*

*(Physical Page 94 / Leaf p96)*

*My Body"*, and *"Drink of it, all of you: this is My Blood"*; and not the words invoking the Holy Spirit, which, as we learn, certain ignorant persons maintain in many catechisms, and following them also in recent observations of schismatic liturgical books (*Liturgikons*) against the ancient faith of the Greek Church. Concerning this truth, among others, Saint John Chrysostom teacheth thus: *"Christ is present now also to adorn the table, because it is not man who causeth the offered gifts to become the Body and Blood of Christ. The priest standeth only fulfilling the figure and offereth prayer: but the grace and power of God it is that accomplisheth all. 'This is My Body' [^103], saith He. This word transubstantiateth the offered gifts. And as that voice: 'Be fruitful, and multiply, and replenish the earth' [^104], was a word and became an act that gave to human nature the power of procreating children, so also this voice uttered, ever multiplieth grace in those who worthily partake"* [^105]. And again: *"For as the words which Christ spoke are the very same which the priest speaketh today, so also the oblation is the very same"* [^106]. Saint Irenaeus: *"When therefore the mingled cup and the broken bread receive the Word of God, it becometh the Eucharist of the Body and Blood of Christ"* [^107]. Saint Gregory of Nyssa: *"Immediately by the Word it is changed into the Body, when the word was spoken: 'This is My Body'"* [^108]. Finally, that true doctrine the entire Church solemnly confessed at the Council of Florence. The Synod likewise insisteth that priests in pronouncing the words of consecration in no wise depart from the received form of individual words wherewith that same consecration is expressed in our present *Liturgikons*; which we have thought necessary to mention here, because it is known to us that one priest in this matter had departed from the common practice.

2. As to the decree of the Synod of Zamość regarding sacred vestments which a priest must use in carrying this Most Holy Mystery to the sick,

*(Physical Page 95 / Leaf p97)*

the said Synod permitteth: *"if the house of the sick person be farther removed from the place where the parish church is situated, to carry Holy Communion secretly, and thereby without church vestments"* [^109]; the present Synod, however, hath thought fit to modify the said decree so far, that a priest carrying the Holy Viaticum (*Sviate Naputie*) to a sick person in this our province shall do so openly and at least according to the common and orderly custom, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, since such custom hath already entered into practice among us.

3. In addition, as to the pattern of prayers which the priest is accustomed to recite for the people to repeat after him when approaching Holy Communion, the Synod admonisheth that all conduct the same exactly as it shall be set forth in the rule above mentioned by this Synod, adding nothing and omitting nothing.

4. During the very action of administering this Holy Mystery, let them not add to the formula: *"The servant of God partaketh"*, the word: *"unworthy"*, as some have been accustomed to add; and after administering Holy Communion to individual communicants, let them not wipe with the towel the holy spoon (*lozhychka*) moistened in the chalice, without a grave reason.

5. But since Holy Communion in our rite is ordinarily administered under both kinds and that during the Divine Liturgy, although under each kind Christ is present whole and entire: at a great concourse of communicating people, let the priest, before the distribution of Holy Communion, never pour unblessed wine into the particles placed in the chalice for the easier distribution of particles placed in large number in the chalice; but let him above all before the Divine Liturgy inquire concerning the number of those who are to receive Communion, and let him pour in a proportionately necessary quantity of wine; and if it should at times happen that an unexpectedly extraordinary number of communicants approach the Holy Table, then let him distribute Holy Communion with that quantity of consecrated wine which he then hath in the chalice; and in the case where particles for Communion in the consecrated chalice should no longer suffice, and there yet remain

*(Physical Page 96 / Leaf p98)*

communicants, then he may administer Holy Communion to them only after the manner of the sick, that is: taking consecrated particles from the pyx (*pushka*) and giving them to those approaching; which latter custom may be observed also in those cases when communicants have not the opportunity to be present at the Divine Liturgy, as especially happeneth in towns, as also on pilgrimages to miraculous places, unto which also not infrequently very late in the afternoon the faithful arrive fasting from distant localities, with the intention of receiving Holy Communion on that same day.

6. Finally, having administered the holy particle to the communicant, let him wipe his mouth with the communion cloth (*ruchnychok*), either himself or the deacon, if there be one; but let him not permit the communicant himself, taking the cloth with his own hand, to wipe his mouth; and it is necessary to wipe it thoroughly, lest accidentally the communicant himself, with his own hand or with his own handkerchief, should wipe his mouth.

7. In fine, regarding the appointed time (*rechyenets*) for Paschal Communion, although it is not unknown to the Synod that according to common law it is comprised within fifteen days, namely in the days from Palm Sunday (*Nedilia Vayi / Tsvitna*) up to Low Sunday (*Antipascha*), called among us Saint Thomas Sunday: — yet since among us the custom hath long since entered, on account of the large number of penitents, to begin that precept from the first day of the Great Fast (Forty Days) and to extend it much later beyond Saint Thomas Sunday: therefore the Synod deemeth it well to retain the custom as to the first term, and as the second term it appointeth the feast of the Ascension of the Lord itself, so that with that feast endeth the second term of Paschal Communion to be received by the faithful, and that in their own parish, from their own pastor, or by his permission and knowledge, in another church and from another priest.

8. If however pastors should learn that anyone of the faithful hath not fulfilled the precept of Paschal Communion, they are bound, after unsuccessful admonitions, to announce to them that the penalty of excommunication existeth, and finally to report such persons to their own Ordinary, who will not fail to issue against the disobedient the penalty of excommunication already decreed at the Synod of Zamość.

---

*(Physical Page 97 / Leaf p99)*

### CHAPTER IV. On the Mystery of Repentance

1. As to the Mystery of Repentance, pastors of souls shall encourage the faithful committed to their solicitude that they receive the same frequently, setting before them earnestly the ancient custom in this matter of the Eastern Church, cited by the Synod of Zamość, that *"at least three times a year, namely at the Resurrection of the Lord (at which time they are bound to do so under penalty of excommunication), at the Dormition of the Most Holy Mother of God, and at the Nativity of Christ the Lord, they approach holy Confession and the Communion of the Divine Body"* [^110]. Although indeed this obligation in later times was relaxed and limited so that these Mysteries should be received at least once a year, around Pascha: nevertheless the present Synod, understanding the importance of the matter, layeth upon pastors the duty to admonish the faithful that they preserve that ancient custom in its integrity, confessing at least three times in the year.

2. The doctrine concerning the precept of annual Confession and Paschal Communion, which in our Catechisms is expounded so that that Confession and Communion should take place specifically before one's own pastor, the present Synod decreeth to modify so that Confession may be made before any priest authorized to hear confessions, but Communion before one's own pastor, or also before another, yet only with the knowledge and permission of one's own pastor. As to the terms of annual Confession, since it is wont to precede Paschal Communion, it is to be comprised within the very same terms as Communion.

3. As to the very administration of this Mystery: whereas in many places the custom hath taken root that the faithful approaching Confession lay down beside the confessor one or two cents (*kraytsary*), and again that on the occasion of Paschal Confession pastors themselves, before issuing to the faithful the confession ticket (*kartka do spovidi*), demand certain dues to be contributed by them under various titles, whether for the pastor (such as *proskurne* and the like), or for the church cantor; and this can very often be an obstacle to willingly approaching Confession, or at least give someone occasion for malicious interpretation: therefore the Synod commandeth that pastors of souls explain to the people in public instructions that for the administration of this Mystery they are not bound to any payment, nor to any offerings. In addition, this Synod forbiddeth pastors in the future, on the occasion of Paschal Confession, to demand either the above-mentioned or any other dues whatever, but to postpone the payment of such to a more suitable time.

*(Physical Page 98 / Leaf p100)*

4. Finally, as to the jurisdiction (*sudovlastia*) of confessors: although according to common law their jurisdiction is limited to their own subjects, yet on account of the custom long since received in our regions and approved by the Bishops of the various rites, they may hear the confessions also of other faithful not subject to them, whether they be of the same or of another rite, as is also declared in the *Concordia*, or whether of their own or of another Eparchy of this Province, without violating however the right of reservation belonging to individual Bishops. Preachers and confessors therefore shall have in their possession a written list of all cases reserved not only to the Pope, as indicated in the Constitution *Apostolicae Sedis* ("Of the Apostolic See") [^111], but also to the Bishops of the three Catholic rites, lest (confessors) through carelessness arrogate authority unto themselves. Yet notwithstanding all this, let confessors know that their jurisdiction doth not extend to monasteries, and especially to monasteries of nuns; only for hearing the confessions of nuns is it necessary to be provided with special approval from the Ordinaries of the respective monasteries.

5. Finally, pastors of souls shall strive that for hearing confessions they have confessional booths (*spovidalnytsi*) in their churches, so that they may receive the confessions of the faithful therein conveniently and decorously.

---

IV, decreeing excommunication reserved to the Roman Pontiff against those who enter the Masonic sect or other secret societies conspiring against the Church or legitimate civil authority, as well as those who favor, conceal, or fail to denounce their leaders.

[^86]: Ruthenian Provincial Synod of Zamość (1720), Titulus II *De praedicatione verbi Dei*, obliging all pastors and preachers to instruct the faithful in the Word of God on all Sundays and solemn feast days.

[^89]: Provincial Council of Vienna (1858), Session IV, Titulus IV, Chapter 4: on conciliar discipline and prudence in sacred preaching, forbidding pastors from venting personal complaints, injuries, or political partisanship from the ambo, and enjoining private fraternal correction rather than public naming of individual sinners.

I, ch. 15), received by the Provincial Council of Prague (1860), Titulus II, Chapter 5 *On Preaching the Word of God*: "Great is the condemnation of him who ordereth his discourse well, but neglecteth his own life and his own works."

[^101]: Ruthenian Provincial Synod of Zamość (1720), Titulus III, § 2 *De Sacramento Confirmationis*, directing that old Holy Chrism be burned upon a clean stone and its ashes reverently cast into the *Sacrarium*.

[^102]: Appendix XVIII (*Додатокъ ч. 18, выпускъ*) of the Synodal Acts: Official liturgical order and rubrics for the Administration of the Mystery of Holy Chrismation (*Ordo Confirmationis*).

I & II; PG 49, 373–392), preached on Great Thursday/Friday, asserting that Christ Himself is present to consecrate the gifts, and that the words "This is My Body" effect the transubstantiation of the Eucharistic offerings.

[^106]: St. John Chrysostom, *Homily II on the Second Epistle to the Corinthians*, no. 4 (PG 61, 393–404), affirming that the Eucharistic sacrifice offered today is identical with that instituted by Christ at the Last Supper.

[^108]: St. Gregory of Nyssa, *Catechetical Oration* (*Oratio Catechetica Magna*), Chapter 37 (PG 45, 93–97), affirming that the Eucharistic bread is immediately transfigured into the Body of the Word through the divine utterance: "This is My Body."

[^109]: Ruthenian Provincial Synod of Zamość (1720), Titulus III, § 3 *De Sacramento Eucharistiae*, regulating the administration of Holy Viaticum to the sick and canonical vestment exemptions for distant journeys.

[^110]: Ruthenian Provincial Synod of Zamość (1720), Titulus III, § 5 *De Sacramento Poenitentiae*, reaffirming the canonical tradition of the Christian East enjoining confession and communion at least three times yearly: at Pascha, the Dormition of the Theotokos, and the Nativity of Christ.

[^111]: Appendix XVIII-b (*Додатокъ ч. 18б*) of the Synodal Acts: Comprehensive catalog of reserved sins and censures (*Casus reservati*) under the Apostolic Constitution *Apostolicae Sedis* (1869) and the diocesan reservation tables of the Ruthenian and Latin ordinaries of Galicia.

<!-- START COHORT 1891_synod_cohort8 -->

# Acts and Decrees of the Ruthenian Provincial Synod of Lviv (1891)
## Tier 1 (The Calibration Anchor) — Cohort 8: Physical Pages 99–118 (Leaves p101–p120)

> [!NOTE]
> **Historical & Canonical Significance**  
> Cohort 8 of the **1891 Lviv Provincial Synod** (*Чинности и рѣшеня руского провинціяльного Собора въ Галичинѣ ôтбувшого ся во Львовѣ въ роцѣ 1891*) encompasses the culmination of the sacramental codification of the Ruthenian Church and the landmark legislation governing sacramentals and public liturgical worship:  
> 1. **Conclusion of Titulus II: On the Mysteries and Their Administration (Leaves p101–p106 / Book pp. 99–104)**:  
>    - **Chapter V: On the Mystery of Anointing (Holy Unction / Euchelaion) (Leaves p101–p102 / Book pp. 99–100)**: Canonical approval of the shorter order (*Chyn "V krattsi"*) introduced by the 1720 Synod of Zamość for general parochial use beyond extreme emergencies, while commending the Greater Order (*Poslidovanie bôlshe*) when multiple priests are present; precise determination of the essential sacramental form words (*"Heal, by this anointing, this Thy servant... giving him life by the grace of Thy Christ"*); detailed anatomical rubrics for unction (thumb of the right hand, cruciformly, dual sense organs starting on the right, eyelids, earlobes, tip of nose, lips, palms, soles); omission of the loins and breasts in women for modesty per the Roman Ritual of Paul V; prohibition of reusing unction oil, mandating that excess oil poured into a shallow silver or pewter vessel be burned in the fire after being wiped with cotton; strict ban on demanding stipends or fees for unction; pastoral duty to summon the priest early while hope of recovery remains, expounding the bodily and spiritual healing effects of the Mystery.  
>    - **Chapter VI: On the Mystery of Priesthood (Holy Orders) (Leaves p103–p105 / Book pp. 101–103)**: Reception of the Tridentine canonical ages (Subdiaconate at 21 completed years, Diaconate at 22, Presbyterate at 24) over ancient canons (Trullo, Carthage, Neo-Caesarea); regulation of interstices (*intervalla temporum*), allowing Reader and Subdeacon on the same day, 1 day between Subdeacon and Deacon, and 8 days between Deacon and Priest; restriction of Diaconal and Priestly ordinations to distinct, non-consecutive solemn feast days; 8-day spiritual exercises (*rekolektsii*) prior to minor orders and holy orders; pre-ordination sacramental confession; mandatory profession of faith according to Pope Urban VIII's formula on the very day of priestly ordination; solemn oath of fidelity to the Roman Pontiff, the local Bishop, church property stewardship, and stability in the Ruthenian Church; individual imposition of hands by the Bishop upon each candidate during the essential form (*"Divine grace..."*).  
>    - **Chapter VII: On the Mystery of Matrimony (Leaves p105–p106 / Book pp. 103–104)**: Observance of Tridentine marriage form, Zamość decrees, and the 1863 *Concordia*; strict episcopal reservation of matrimonial dispensations; warning against illegal rite-switching from the Latin to Greek rite and invalidity of marriages celebrated without the *parochus proprius*; pastoral vigilance and enforcement of Pope Gregory XVI's 1841/1842 Apostolic Letters and Holy Office Decrees of 1888 on mixed marriages; canonical timing of nuptials (morning hours before noon following confession and communion); strict adherence to the 1868 joint pastoral instruction of Metropolitan Spyrydon Lytvynovych and Bishop Joseph Sembratovych on Austrian civil marriage legislation.  
> 2. **Promulgation of Titulus III: On Consecrations and Blessings — (Sacramentals) (Leaves p106–p110 / Book pp. 104–108)**:  
>    - **Theological Nature & Ecclesial Dignity**: Distinction between Sacraments (*ex opere operato*) and Sacramentals (*ex opere operantis*); higher prominence and popular esteem of sacramentals in the Ruthenian Church compared to contemporary Greek practice; St. Thomas Aquinas (ST III, q. 87, a. 8) on the remission of venial sins and collation of actual grace through ecclesiastical blessings.  
>    - **Exorcisms & Childbirth Purification**: Strict medical-pastoral investigation of demonic possession versus natural psychic/physical illness; prohibition of immediate or public exorcisms without prior episcopal report and express license; reform of churching after childbirth (*ochyshchenie po rodzheniu*), strictly excluding women who bore illegitimate children to deter from public scandal and substituting sacramental penance; establishing a minimum 20-day convalescence period (ideally 40 days per the *Trebnik*) to prevent rural women from resuming crushing field labor prematurely.  
>    - **Blessings of Elements & Buildings**: Solemn Artoklasia (*Blahoslovenie khlibiv*) at All-Night Vigils, with distribution of blessed bread to the faithful during anointing, pouring leftover oil into church lamps, and reserving wheat and wine for the clergy; episcopal delegation faculties for simple priests to bless foundations, churches, altars, bell towers, bells, and cemeteries using the simple priest's ritual; enumeration of sacred vessels and vestments reserved to the Bishop (antimension, chalice, tabernacle, diskos, spoon, monstrance); comprehensive reform of the blessing of a new house, abolishing the dangerous custom of singeing walls with candles and mandating cotton wiping and burning of blessed oil; Great and Lesser Water Blessings, mandating the primary liturgical commemoration of the Pope of Rome (*"Universal Pontiff"*) alongside the Emperor as codified in historic Pochaiv editions.  
> 3. **Promulgation of Titulus IV: On the Public Worship of God (Leaves p110–p120 / Book pp. 108–118)**:  
>    - **Theology of Rites & Catholic Unity**: Dual nature of man requiring public social worship; Old Testament divine institution and New Testament Christological foundation; the Church clad in the seamless garment of faith and the many-colored robe of diverse liturgical rites; St. Augustine on Psalm 44:10 (*"In veste sit varietas, scissura non sit"*); Pope Pius IX's 1862 Encyclical and Pope Leo XIII's 1888 allocution affirming that the Eastern rites augment the apostolic beauty, dignity, and majesty of the Catholic Church; division into Sacrifice and the Divine Office.  
>    - **Chapter I: On the Sacrifice of the Liturgy (Divine Liturgy) (Leaves p111–p116 / Book pp. 109–114)**: The threefold Byzantine Liturgy (St. Basil the Great, St. John Chrysostom, Presanctified of St. Gregory Dialogist); pastoral obligation to celebrate St. Basil 10 times yearly and St. John Chrysostom on all Sundays/feasts; exhortation to celebrate weekday low Liturgies without stipends based on patristic authorities (St. Thomas Aquinas, St. John Chrysostom, Timothy of Jerusalem, Venerable Bede); statutory calendar of the Presanctified Gifts (Wednesdays and Fridays of Great Lent, first three days of Holy Week) and retention of St. John Chrysostom on Lenten Mondays, Tuesdays, and Thursdays; aliturgical days (Great Friday, Eve of Christmas/Theophany when Royal Hours are transferred); Vesperal Liturgies; celebrant preparation (cassock/soutane, absolute Eucharistic fasting from midnight under mortal sin, refuting the 6-hour error, tobacco fasting from midnight); purity of conscience and mandatory confession per Trent Sess. 13, c. 7; canonical prayer preparation (Matins with 1st Hour); liturgical decorum and duration (low Mass 30–45 minutes); concelebration rules (simultaneous quiet pronunciation of the consecration form words, silent prayers, distinction between assisting and concelebrating clergy); standard parish Liturgy hour (10:00 AM); post-liturgical 15-minute thanksgiving.  
>    - **Chapter II: On the Intention and Stipends of Divine Liturgies (Leaves p116–p120 / Book pp. 114–118)**: The *Missa pro populo* obligation binding bishops and pastors on all Sundays and holy days of obligation, including suppressed feast days (*feriae abrogatae* per Pius IX *Amantissimi Redemptoris* 1856) and feasts transferred to Sunday per Leo XIII (1886); absolute prohibition of Mass stipends for the Presanctified Gifts; foundation Masses and strict adherence to foundation charters (Innocent XII *Nuper* 1697); manual stipends, prohibition of simony, bargaining, or angling for smaller stipends for shameful gain (*turpe lucrum* per Benedict XIV *Quanta cura* 1741 and Zamość); full remittance of transferred manual stipends (Urban VIII *Cum saepe* 1625); distinction of foundation endowments and stole fees (*congrua* and *iura epitrachelialia* per Sacred Congregation of the Council); limitation of stipend accumulation to 1–2 months; prohibition of unauthorized reduction or amalgamation of small stipends; mandatory parish Mass registers (*Liber Missarum*).  
>    - **Chapter III: On the Canonical Hours (Commencement) (Leaf p120 / Book p. 118)**: Enumeration of the eight parts of the Byzantine Divine Office (*Pravylo Tserkovne*); role of the Typika (*Obidnytsia*); All-Night Vigil rubrics (Vespers joined with Matins, omitting Compline and Midnight Office, prescribing Small Vespers).

   - [Section 4: On the Blessing of Churches, Altars, Bell Towers, and Cemeteries (Leaf p108 / Book p. 106)](#section-4-on-the-blessing-of-churches-altars-bell-towers-and-cemeteries)
   - [Section 5: On the Blessing of Sacred Vestments, Vessels, and Furnishings (Leaf p109 / Book p. 107)](#section-5-on-the-blessing-of-sacred-vestments-vessels-and-furnishings)
   - [Section 6: On the Blessing of a New House and the Consecration of Water (Leaves p109–p110 / Book pp. 107–108)](#section-6-on-the-blessing-of-a-new-house-and-the-consecration-of-water)
